<div class='question question'>

%waffles%

$3256 \div 4$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3256 \div 4 \\\\
&= \dfrac{3256}{4} \\\\
&= \dfrac{814 \times 4}{4} \\\\
&= \dfrac{814 \times \cancel 4}{\cancel 4} \\\\
&= 814
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$814$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3256 \div 4 \\\\
&= \dfrac{3256}{4} \\\\
&= \dfrac{814 \times 4}{4} \\\\
&= \dfrac{814 \times \cancel 4}{\cancel 4} \\\\
&= 814
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7E1A8AB3-C593-4477-9220-607D3E76FDBE'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find the difference between $2.7 \ \text{metres}$ and $32 \ \text{centimetres}$.

Give your answer in metres.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required
. 

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2.7 \ \text{m} - 32 \ \text{cm} \\\\
&= 2.7 \ \text{m} - \dfrac{32}{100} \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 2.7 \ \text{m} - 0.32 \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 2.38 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2.38 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_no_dot_note. 
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2.7 \ \text{m} - 32 \ \text{cm} \\\\
&= 2.7 \ \text{m} - \dfrac{32}{100} \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 2.7 \ \text{m} - 0.32 \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 2.38 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2.38 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B5B49362-C409-4628-B777-B5B39EC20042'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

What fraction of a minute is $40 \ \text{seconds}$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

  
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{40}{60} \\\\
&= \dfrac{20 \times 2}{20 \times 3} \\\\
&= \dfrac{\cancel{20} \times 2}{\cancel{20} \times 3} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2}{3}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{2}{3}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>  
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{40}{60} \\\\
&= \dfrac{20 \times 2}{20 \times 3} \\\\
&= \dfrac{\cancel{20} \times 2}{\cancel{20} \times 3} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2}{3}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{2}{3}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-53FB1D0A-B461-448E-853F-B08C8593DFC5'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Put the following fractions in order, starting with the smallest.

$\dfrac{2}{3},  \quad \dfrac{3}{5},   \quad \dfrac{29}{45}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

To compare, we can convert all the numbers to fractions, decimals or percentages. 

Let's convert all the numbers into fractions.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{2}{3},  \quad \dfrac{3}{5},   \quad \dfrac{29}{45} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2 \times 15}{3 \times 15},  \quad \dfrac{3 \times 9}{5 \times 9},   \quad \dfrac{29}{45} \\\\
&= \dfrac{30}{45},  \quad \dfrac{27}{45},   \quad \dfrac{29}{45}
\end{aligned}
$

Now let's put them in ascending order.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{27}{45},  \quad \dfrac{29}{45},   \quad \dfrac{30}{45} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3}{5},  \quad \dfrac{29}{45},   \quad \dfrac{2}{3} 
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{3}{5},  \quad \dfrac{29}{45},   \quad \dfrac{2}{3}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>To compare, we can convert all the numbers to fractions, decimals or percentages. 
<emptyline>
Let's convert all the numbers into fractions.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{2}{3},  \quad \dfrac{3}{5},   \quad \dfrac{29}{45} \\\\
&= \dfrac{2 \times 15}{3 \times 15},  \quad \dfrac{3 \times 9}{5 \times 9},   \quad \dfrac{29}{45} \\\\
&= \dfrac{30}{45},  \quad \dfrac{27}{45},   \quad \dfrac{29}{45}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Now let's put them in ascending order.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{27}{45},  \quad \dfrac{29}{45},   \quad \dfrac{30}{45} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3}{5},  \quad \dfrac{29}{45},   \quad \dfrac{2}{3} 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{3}{5},  \quad \dfrac{29}{45},   \quad \dfrac{2}{3}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Which is the smallest number?

$0.54     \quad 0.092  \quad    0.635  \quad    0.3$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the Question,

$0.54, \quad 0.092, \quad 0.635, \quad 0.3$

lets Arrange in ascending order.

$0.092, \quad 0.3, \quad 0.54, \quad 0.635$

The  smallest number is $= 0.092$. 

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.092$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the Question,
<emptyline>
$0.54, \quad 0.092, \quad 0.635, \quad 0.3$
<emptyline>
lets Arrange in ascending order.
<emptyline>
$0.092, \quad 0.3, \quad 0.54, \quad 0.635$
<emptyline>
The  smallest number is $= 0.092$. 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

Which number between $55$ and $65$ can be divided exactly by $9$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$63$

</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-C8C3BB23-7980-4A19-8082-5F985DD48CAC'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Which number between $140$ and $170$ can be divided exactly by both $8$ and $9$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

The LCM of $8$ and $9$ is $72$. 

Now let's find a number that is a multiple of the LCM and is between $140$ and $170$.

$72 \times 2 = 144$ is between $140$ and $170$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$144$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>The LCM of $8$ and $9$ is $72$. 
<emptyline>
Now let's find a number that is a multiple of the LCM and is between $140$ and $170$.
<emptyline>
$72 \times 2 = 144$ is between $140$ and $170$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$144$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DB188EF1-56DE-4E7B-9F6F-B9D010FD69AF'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Add together the following numbers and write your answer in figures.

$\text{nine million      \quad   eleven thousand \quad       twelve hundred     \quad   thirteen}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 9000000 + 11000 + 1200 + 13 \\\\
&= 9012213
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9012213$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 9000000 + 11000 + 1200 + 13 \\\\
&= 9012213
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-280A1E97-7786-4101-AFA7-0DE251344677'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>


The diagram shows the distances between some cities.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


If the total distance travelled from Lisbon to Munich is $2730 \ \text{km}$, how far was the Journey from Madrid to Paris?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the distance between Madrid to Paris $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2730      &= 638 + a + 827 \\\\
2730      &= 1465 + a \\\\
1465 + a  &= 2730 \\\\
a         &= 2730 - 1465 \\\\
a         &= 1265 \ \text{km}
\end{aligned}
$

The distance between Madrid to Paris $= 1265 \ \text{km}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1265 \ \text{km}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the distance between Madrid to Paris $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2730      &= 638 + a + 827 \\\\
2730      &= 1465 + a \\\\
1465 + a  &= 2730 \\\\
a         &= 2730 - 1465 \\\\
a         &= 1265 \ \text{km}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The distance between Madrid to Paris $= 1265 \ \text{km}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1265 \ \text{km}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the least number of coins she could use to reach the exact price?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\pounds 2.28    &= \pounds 2 + 20 \text {p} + 5 \text {p} + 2 \text {p} + 1 \text {p}
\end {aligned}
$

Least number of coins she can use $= 5$ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\pounds 2.28    &= \pounds 2 + 20 \text {p} + 5 \text {p} + 2 \text {p} + 1 \text {p}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Least number of coins she can use $= 5$ 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5$ 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Miriam pays for two of these magazines with a $\pounds 10$ note. How much change should she receive?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total cost}      &= 2 \times 2.28 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 4.56 \\\\
\text {Change received} &= 10 - 4.56 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 5.44 
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 5.44$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total cost}      &= 2 \times 2.28 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 4.56 \\\\
\text {Change received} &= 10 - 4.56 \\\\
                        &= \pounds 5.44 
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 5.44$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

Complete the table of values for this number machine with the rule 'multiply by $4$, then subtract $3$'.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Number IN $= 10$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 10 \times 4 - 3 \\\\
&= 40 - 3 \\\\
&= 37
\end{aligned}
$

Number OUT $= 81$

Let input number $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times a - 3 &= 81 \\\\
4 \times a &= 81 + 3 \\\\
4 \times a &= 84 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{84}{4} \\\\
a &= \dfrac{21 \times \cancel 4}{\cancel 4} \\\\
a &= 21
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='answer'>

$37 \\\\ 21$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the rule for this number machine? Write your answer inside the machine.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the rule $= \text{Multiply by}\ a \ \text{then add}\ b$

Input $= 2$

Output $= 17$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + b &= 17 \\\\
2a + b &= 17
\end{aligned}
$

Input $= 7$

Output $= 52$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 7 + b &= 52 \\\\
7a + b &= 52
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for $a$

$
\begin{aligned}
7a + b &= 52 \\\\
7a + (17 - 2a) &= 52 \\\\
7a - 2a &= 52 - 17 \\\\
5a &= 35 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{35}{5} \\\\
a &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for $b$

$
\begin{aligned}
2a + b &= 17 \\\\
2 \times 7 + b &= 17 \\\\
14 + b &= 17 \\\\
b &= 17 - 14 \\\\
b &= 3 \\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets verify,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 9 \times 7 + 3 \\\\
&= 63 + 3 \\\\
&= \green {66} = 66 \\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 10 \times 7 + 3 \\\\
&= 70 + 3 \\\\
&= \green {73} = 73 \\\
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the rule $= \text{Multipy by}\ 7 \ \text{then add}\ 3$

</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-8CA44EE7-F16E-4F90-964D-A9A239631F65'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>


On her birthday last year, Emily was $140\ \text{cm}$ tall. When she measured herself on her birthday today, she calculated that she had grown by a fifth of the height she was a year ago.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOANSWER
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How tall is she now?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the new hieght of Emily $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a 				&= (1 + \dfrac{1}{5}) \times 140  \\\\
a 				&= (\dfrac{1 \times 5}{1 \times 5} + \dfrac{1}{5}) \times 140 \\\\
a 				&= (\dfrac{5 + 1}{5}) \times 140 \\\\
a 				&= \dfrac{6}{5} \times 140  \\\\
a 				&= \dfrac {6 \times 140}{5} \\\\
a 				&= \dfrac{6 \times 28 \times 5}{5} \\\\
a 				&= \dfrac{6 \times 28 \times \cancel 5}{\cancel 5} \\\\
a 				&= 6 \times 28 \\\\
a 				&= 168 \text{ cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Emily is now $168 \ \text{cm}$ tall.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$168 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the new hieght of Emily $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a 				&= (1 + \dfrac{1}{5}) \times 140  \\\\
a 				&= (\dfrac{1 \times 5}{1 \times 5} + \dfrac{1}{5}) \times 140 \\\\
a 				&= (\dfrac{5 + 1}{5}) \times 140 \\\\
a 				&= \dfrac{6}{5} \times 140  \\\\
a 				&= \dfrac {6 \times 140}{5} \\\\
a 				&= \dfrac{6 \times 28 \times 5}{5} \\\\
a 				&= \dfrac{6 \times 28 \times \cancel 5}{\cancel 5} \\\\
a 				&= 6 \times 28 \\\\
a 				&= 168 \text{ cm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Emily is now $168 \ \text{cm}$ tall.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$168 \ \text{cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Emily's brother, Jack, is now $105\ \text{cm}$ tall, having grown by one sixth of his height a year ago. How tall was Jack one year ago?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let one year ago Jacks height $= a$

As per the question

$
\begin{aligned}
a + \dfrac{1}{6} \times a 										  &= 105 \\\\
a + \dfrac{a}{6}                                                  &= 105 \\\\
\dfrac{a \times 6}{1 \times 6} + \dfrac{a}{6}                     &= 105 \\\\
\dfrac{6a + 1a}{6}                                                &= 105 \\\\
\dfrac{7a}{6}                                                     &= 105 \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac{105 \times 6}{7} \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac{15 \times 7 \times 6}{7} \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac{15 \times \cancel 7 \times 6}{\cancel 7} \\\\
a                                                                 &= 90 \text { cm}
\end{aligned}
$

One year ago Jack was $90 \text { cm}$ tall.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$90 \text { cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let one year ago Jacks height $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + \dfrac{1}{6} \times a 										  &= 105 \\\\
a + \dfrac{a}{6}                                                  &= 105 \\\\
\dfrac{a \times 6}{1 \times 6} + \dfrac{a}{6}                     &= 105 \\\\
\dfrac{6a + 1a}{6}                                                &= 105 \\\\
\dfrac{7a}{6}                                                     &= 105 \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac{105 \times 6}{7} \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac{15 \times 7 \times 6}{7} \\\\
a                                                                 &= \dfrac{15 \times \cancel 7 \times 6}{\cancel 7} \\\\
a                                                                 &= 90 \text { cm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
One year ago Jack was $90 \text { cm}$ tall.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-3B49E997-8A30-4C39-B5A0-263307F6E8C8'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

$32$ students and $4$ members of staff from Encrypt School are going to a lecture on code-breaking.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Code-Breaking Lecture

Ticket Prices

Students: $\pounds 14.00$ each
Adults: $\pounds 15.00$ each

$1$ free adult ticket provided with every $10$ student tickets purchased

How much will the tickets cost in total for the school party?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total cost}  &= 32 \times 14 + (4 - 3) \times 15 \\\\
                    &= 32 \times 14 + 1 \times 15 \\\\
                    &= 448 + 15 \\\\
                    &= \pounds 463
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 463$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total cost}  &= 32 \times 14 + (4 - 3) \times 15 \\\\
                    &= 32 \times 14 + 1 \times 15 \\\\
                    &= 448 + 15 \\\\
                    &= \pounds 463
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 463$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-23922083-1A2A-4C9C-B1E2-EB1A65CFD5A5'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>


The time in Athens is two hours ahead of the time in London.
For example when it is $15{:}00$ in London it is $17{:}00$ in Athens.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOANSWER
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Nani flies directly to Athens from London, departing at $08{:}20$ hours. 
If the journey from London takes $3 \ \text{hours } 45 \ \text{minutes}$, at what time, local to Athens, will Nani arrive in Athens?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time to Athens}               &= 08{:}20 + 3 \ \text{hours } 45 \ \text{minutes} + 2 \ \text{hours } \\\\
                                    &= 08{:}20 + 5 \ \text{hours } 45 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                    &= 14{:}05
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14{:}05$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time to Athens}               &= 08{:}20 + 3 \ \text{hours } 45 \ \text{minutes} + 2 \ \text{hours } \\\\
                                    &= 08{:}20 + 5 \ \text{hours } 45 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                    &= 14{:}05
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$14{:}05$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

It takes the same time to fly from Athens to London as it takes from London to Athens.

Iani leaves Athens airport at $19{:}15$ hours.

At what time will she reach London (local time)?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time Nani arrives in London}     &= 19{:}15 + 3 \ \text{hours } 45 \ \text{minutes} - 2 \ \text{hours } \\\\
                                    &= 19{:}15 + 1 \ \text{hour } 45 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                    &= 21{:}00
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$21{:}00$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time Nani arrives in London}     &= 19{:}15 + 3 \ \text{hours } 45 \ \text{minutes} - 2 \ \text{hours } \\\\
                                    &= 19{:}15 + 1 \ \text{hour } 45 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                    &= 21{:}00
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-C56E63F7-3A43-43A3-BC1A-1FC3E09AE47C'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

What is the area of this shape?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


ABBR: $\text{Area of triangle} = \dfrac{1}{2} \times \text{base} \times \text{height}$


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


*TODO:: Show the diagram divided into rectangle and triangle with the calculations for the parts of the shape which do not have measurements shown.*

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


As per the diagram, the shape can be divided into a triangle and a rectangle.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area}     &= \text{Area of the triangle} + \text{Area of the rectangle} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 4 \times 4 + 2 \times 4 \\\\
                &= \dfrac {4 \times 4} {2} + 8 \\\\
                &= \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 4} {2} + 8 \\\\
                &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 2 \times 4} {\cancel {2}} + 8 \\\\
                &= 2 \times 4 + 8 \\\\
                &= 8 + 8 \\\\
                &= 16 \ \text{units}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16 \ \text{units}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_triangle_expanded
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_add_drawing_starts
*TODO:: Show the diagram divided into rectangle and triangle with the calculations for the parts of the shape which do not have measurements shown.*
rtq_abbr_md_add_drawing_ends
<emptyline>
As per the diagram, the shape can be divided into a triangle and a rectangle.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area}     &= \text{Area of the triangle} + \text{Area of the rectangle} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 4 \times 4 + 2 \times 4 \\\\
                &= \dfrac {4 \times 4} {2} + 8 \\\\
                &= \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 4} {2} + 8 \\\\
                &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 2 \times 4} {\cancel {2}} + 8 \\\\
                &= 2 \times 4 + 8 \\\\
                &= 8 + 8 \\\\
                &= 16 \ \text{units}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$16 \ \text{units}^2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-126BE312-5DB7-4CD7-BD6E-ED83FD5EC448'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

It takes me half an hour to fill the paddling pool to a depth of $20\ \text{cm}$ using one hose.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOANSWER
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long will it take to fill it to the same depth If I use two hoses?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{hose fills } 20 \ \text{cm in} &= 1 \ \text{hour} \\\\
2 \ \text{hose will fill } 20 \ \text{cm in} &= \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{hour}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {2}$ hour

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{hose fills } 20 \ \text{cm in} &= 1 \ \text{hour} \\\\
2 \ \text{hose will fill } 20 \ \text{cm in} &= \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{hour}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {2}$ hour
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long will it take to fill it to a depth of $30\ \text{cm}$ if I use $3$ hoses?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{hose fills } 20 \ \text{cm in}         											&= 1 \ \text{hour} \\\\
1 \ \text{hose will fill } 10 \ \text{cm in}     											&= \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{hour} \\\\
1 \ \text{hose will fill } 30 \ \text{cm in}     											&= \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{hour} \\\\
3 \text{ hose will fill } 30 \text{ cm in} 													&= \dfrac{1}{3} \times (1 + \dfrac{1}{2}) \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{1}{3}  + \dfrac{1}{3 \times 2} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{1 \times 2}{3 \times 2}  + \dfrac{1}{3 \times 2} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{2}{6}  + \dfrac{1}{6} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{2 + 1}{6} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{3}{6} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{3}{3 \times 2} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{\cancel{3}}{\cancel{3} \times 2} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{1}{2} \text{ hour}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {2}$ hour

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{hose fills } 20 \ \text{cm in}         											&= 1 \ \text{hour} \\\\
1 \ \text{hose will fill } 10 \ \text{cm in}     											&= \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{hour} \\\\
1 \ \text{hose will fill } 30 \ \text{cm in}     											&= \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{hour} \\\\
3 \text{ hose will fill } 30 \text{ cm in} 													&= \dfrac{1}{3} \times (1 + \dfrac{1}{2}) \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{1}{3}  + \dfrac{1}{3 \times 2} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{1 \times 2}{3 \times 2}  + \dfrac{1}{3 \times 2} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{2}{6}  + \dfrac{1}{6} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{2 + 1}{6} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{3}{6} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{3}{3 \times 2} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{\cancel{3}}{\cancel{3} \times 2} \\\\
																							&= \dfrac{1}{2} \text{ hour}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {2}$ hour
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

What fraction of this diagram is shaded?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total triangles}  &= 16   \\\\
\text{Shaded}           &= 3    \\\\
\text{Fraction shaded}  &= \dfrac {3} {16}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

$\dfrac {3} {16}$

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What are the values of $x$ and $y$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 + x + 2           &= 8 \\\\
x + 6               &= 8 \\\\
x                   &= 8 - 6 \\\\
x                   &= 2 \ \text {cm} \\\\
y + (6 - 2 - 2) + y &= 14 \\\\
2y + 2              &= 14 \\\\
2y                  &= 14 - 2 \\\\
2y                  &= 12 \\\\
y                   &= \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
y                   &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
y                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 6} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
y                   &= 6 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$x = 2 \ \text {cm} \ , y = 6 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the diagram,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4 + x + 2           &= 8 \\\\
x + 6               &= 8 \\\\
x                   &= 8 - 6 \\\\
x                   &= 2 \ \text {cm} \\\\
y + (6 - 2 - 2) + y &= 14 \\\\
2y + 2              &= 14 \\\\
2y                  &= 14 - 2 \\\\
2y                  &= 12 \\\\
y                   &= \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
y                   &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
y                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 6} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
y                   &= 6 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$x = 2 \ \text {cm} \ , y = 6 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the perimetre of shape?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of polygon} = \text{Sum of all sides}$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}       &= 2 + 2 + 2 + 6 + 4 + 14 + 4 + 6 + 2 + 2 + 2 + 6 \\\\
                        &= 52 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$52 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the area of the shape?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


*TODO:: Show the diagram divided into three rectangles the calculations for the sides of the shape which do not have measurements shown*

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


As per the diagram, the shape can be divided into three rectangles.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area}     &= \text{Rectangle 1} + \text{Rectangle 2} + \text {Rectangle 3} \\\\
                &= 6 \times 2 + 2 \times 2 + 14 \times 4 \\\\
                &= 12 + 4 + 56 \\\\
                &= 72 \ \text{m}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$72 \ \text{m}^2$

</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>


Nine identical squares are glued together to form the figure.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


The perimeter of the figure is $160\ \text{cm}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

TODOANSWER

</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the length of the side of each small square.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the length of the side $= a$

Number of sides included in the shape $= 16$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
16 \times a             &= 160 \\\\
16a                     &= 160 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {160} {16} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {16 \times 10} {16} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {16} \times 10} {\cancel {16}} \\\\
a                       &= 10 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the side of each small square $= 10 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the length of the side $= a$

a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {16} \times 10} {\cancel {16}} \\\\
a                       &= 10 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Length of the side of each small square $= 10 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$10 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the area of each small square.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of a square}            &= 10 \times 10 \\\\
                                    &= 100 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-AEF8D987-2111-4062-8FFB-061D2FAA0A7F'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Amir, Anna and Arjun have bought some fruit at a market stall.

Amir bought $2$ Pears and $1$ Mango for $\pounds 1.35$

Anna bought $1$ Pear and $2$ Mangos for $\pounds 1.20$

Arjun bought $1$ Pear, $1$ Mango and $1$ Apple for $\pounds 1$

How much, in pence, did the apple cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the price of pear $= p$

Let the price of Mango $= m$

Let the price of apple $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2p + m    &= 1.35 \\\\
p + 2m    &= 1.20 \\\\
p + m + a &= 1
\end{aligned}
$

For calculating the cost of apple we will have to use elimination method. So lets add eqaution 1 and equation 2 first

$
\begin{aligned}
2p + m + p + 2m   &= 1.35 + 1.20 \\\\
3p + 3m           &= 2.55 \\\\
3(p + m)          &= 2.55 \\\\
p + m             &= \dfrac{2.55}{3} \\\\
p + m             &= 0.85
\end{aligned}
$

Now we have,

$
\begin{aligned}
p + m + a         &= 1 \\\\
p + m             &= 0.85 
\end{aligned}
$

So now, using elimination method on the above two equations

$
\begin{aligned}
p + m + a - (p + m) &= 1 - 0.85 \\\\
p + m + a - p - m   &= 1 - 0.85 \\\\
a                   &= \pounds 0.15 \\\\
a                   &= 0.15 \times 100 \text{p} \\\\ 
a                   &= 15 \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the cost of an apple $= 15$ p


</div>
</div>

</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of ladies lost their gloves?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of ladies lost their gloves}         &= \dfrac {5} {5 + 13} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {5} {18}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {5} {18}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of ladies lost their gloves}         &= \dfrac {5} {5 + 13} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {5} {18}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$52$ ladies did not lose their gloves.

Some ladies lost one glove and some lost both gloves. 

After the party, the $29$ missing gloves were found.

How many ladies lost only one glove?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of ladies who lost only one glove $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of ladies lost their gloves}         &= 72 - 52 \\\\
                                                  &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + (20 - a) \times 2                 &= 29 \\\\
a + 20 \times 2 - 2a                  &= 29 \\\\
a + 40 - 2a                           &= 29 \\\\
40 - 2a + a                           &= 29 \\\\
40 - a                                &= 29 \\\\
40 - 29                               &= a \\\\
11                                    &= a \\\\
a                                     &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of ladies who lost only one glove $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of ladies lost their gloves}         &= 72 - 52 \\\\
                                                  &= 20
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + (20 - a) \times 2                 &= 29 \\\\
a + 20 \times 2 - 2a                  &= 29 \\\\
a + 40 - 2a                           &= 29 \\\\
40 - 2a + a                           &= 29 \\\\
40 - a                                &= 29 \\\\
40 - 29                               &= a \\\\
11                                    &= a \\\\
a                                     &= 11
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DAFC965B-0C09-46A4-9759-E2AD3E3F5A28'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Dominic, Hannahand Abdul have $36$ sweets between them.

Dominic has half as many sweets as Hannah.

Abdul has $3$ times as many sweets as Hannah.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


How many sweets does Abdul have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of sweets with Hannah $= a$

Then the number of sweets with Dominic $= \dfrac{a}{2}$

The number of sweets with Abdul $= 3a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + \dfrac{a}{2} + 3a                                  &= 36 \\\\
4a +\dfrac{a}{2}                                       &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{4a \times 2}{1 \times 2}  +\dfrac{a}{2}         &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{8a}{2}  +\dfrac{a}{2}                           &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{8a + a}{2}                                      &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{9a}{2}                                          &= 36 \\\\
a                                                      &= \dfrac{36 \times 2}{9} \\\\
a                                                      &= \dfrac{9\times 4 \times 2}{9} \\\\
a                                                      &= \dfrac{\cancel 9\times 4 \times 2}{\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                                      &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of sweets Abdul had} &= 3 \times a \\\\
								  &= 3 \times 8 \\\\
  							      &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of sweets with Hannah $= a$
<emptyline>
Then the number of sweets with Dominic $= \dfrac{a}{2}$
<emptyline>
The number of sweets with Abdul $= 3a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + \dfrac{a}{2} + 3a                                  &= 36 \\\\
4a +\dfrac{a}{2}                                       &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{4a \times 2}{1 \times 2}  +\dfrac{a}{2}         &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{8a}{2}  +\dfrac{a}{2}                           &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{8a + a}{2}                                      &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{9a}{2}                                          &= 36 \\\\
a                                                      &= \dfrac{36 \times 2}{9} \\\\
a                                                      &= \dfrac{9\times 4 \times 2}{9} \\\\
a                                                      &= \dfrac{\cancel 9\times 4 \times 2}{\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                                      &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of sweets Abdul had} &= 3 \times a \\\\
								  &= 3 \times 8 \\\\
  							      &= 24
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$24$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-E2CE5C41-401D-471B-B868-C50B3E4DD99A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

If $a$ and $b$ are numbers, then $a \ \hearts \ b$ means **'multiply $a$ by itself and then subtract $b$'**.

For example

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \hearts \  3 &= 2 \times 2 - 3 \\
                 &= 4 - 3 \\
                 &= 1
\end{aligned}
$


</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

</div>

\begin{aligned}
&= 3 \times 3 - 4 \\\\
&= 9 - 4 \\\\
&= 5
\end{aligned}
$

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find $a$ when $a \ \hearts \ 4 = 32$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times a - 4    &= 32 \\\\
a \times a        &= 32 + 4 \\\\
a^2               &= 36 \\\\
a                 &= \sqrt{36} \\\\
a                 &= 6 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
a \times a - 4    &= 32 \\\\
a \times a        &= 32 + 4 \\\\
a^2               &= 36 \\\\
a                 &= \sqrt{36} \\\\
a                 &= 6 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find $x$ when $x \ \hearts \ x = 72$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
x \times x - x         &= 72 \\\\
x \times (x - 1)       &= 72 
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

- The product of the $2$ numbers is $72$ 
- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $1$.

Factors of $72 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 9, 12, 18, 24, 36, 72$

Factor pairs of $72$ that have difference of $1$ = (1, 2), (2, 3), (3, 4), (8 ,9)

| Factors   |  Difference  |    Product       |
|:-------:  |:-----------: |:---------------: |
| $1, 2$    |    $1$       |       2          |
| $2, 3$    |    $1$       |       6          |
| $3, 4$    |    $1$       |       12         |
| $8, 9$    |    $1$       |       72         |

A quick check shows that $8$ and $9$ meets all the criteria above.

*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. ABBR: Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.
*

The two numbers are $= 8, 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8, 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
x \times x - x         &= 72 \\\\
x \times (x - 1)       &= 72 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.
<emptyline>
- The product of the $2$ numbers is $72$ 
- The difference of the $2$ numbers is $1$.
<emptyline>
Factors of $72 = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 9, 12, 18, 24, 36, 72$
<emptyline>
Factor pairs of $72$ that have difference of $1$ = (1, 2), (2, 3), (3, 4), (8 ,9)
<emptyline>
| Factors   |  Difference  |    Product       |
|:-------:  |:-----------: |:---------------: |
| $1, 2$    |    $1$       |       2          |
| $2, 3$    |    $1$       |       6          |
| $3, 4$    |    $1$       |       12         |
| $8, 9$    |    $1$       |       72         |
<emptyline>
A quick check shows that $8$ and $9$ meets all the criteria above.
<emptyline>
*Note: The two equations form a quadratic equation. rtq_abbr_md_quadratic_note*
<emptyline>
The two numbers are $= 8, 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8, 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-51656C94-CF31-4FBB-94BD-BDD4145FBB17'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Each letter from $A$ to $I$ is a code for one of the digits $1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8$ or $9$. 
We already know that $H = 2$ and $I = 7$

$H + I = 9$

$H \times I = 14$

and $HI$ means $27$

Now crack the rest of the code using the following:

$G \times C = 30$ 

$D + D = A$

$D \times D = BC$

$D + E = BF$

$E + E = BA$

$E \times E = AB$

$D \times E = FC$

</div>
